日本：旺盛なAI関連需要が製造業の景況感を押し上げ。利益計画は大幅上方修正
3
 大企業の業況判断は製造業が6四半期連続の改善。非製造業は悪化も一時的な悪天候が主因で変調の懸念は低い
― 業種別では、化学、石油・石炭製品、生産用機械等が改善。旺盛なAI関連需要や原油の供給不足懸念の後退等が背景

― 一方、非製造業は、長雨を受けて小売や対個人サービス、宿泊・飲食サービスが悪化
 2026年度の経常利益計画は、製造業中心に大幅上方修正。9月調査時点としては過去5年間で最高の増益計画
― とりわけ大企業・製造業では、輸出向け売上高の上方修正が顕著。上期の実績が大幅に上振れたことを反映したもの
― 世界的なAI関連需要の好調持続が見込まれるもと、下期も堅調な輸出が続けば、利益計画は一段の上方修正余地

日本：設備投資は高水準を維持。製造業は業績対比でなお上振れ余地
4
 2026年度の設備投資計画は前年度比＋9.3％と高い伸びを維持
― 製造業は6月調査からほぼ横ばいの同＋7.1％。非製造業は同＋11.8％と1.1％Ptの上方修正
 AI需要による好業績を踏まえれば製造業の投資計画にはやや慎重さがにじむ内容
― 一方、生産・営業用設備判断DIは2025年度後半以降、設備不足方向への動きが鮮明。高水準の内部資金も踏まえれ
ば、増産投資などを中心に今後の設備投資が上振れる余地
― なお、今後の利上げが企業の投資姿勢に及ぼす影響には留意
25年度後半以降、急速
な設備不足超を示唆

日本：企業の価格設定姿勢はなお強い。仕入価格再上昇にも警戒
5
 大企業・製造業の販売価格判断DIは前期並みの高水準を維持。企業は積極的な価格転嫁姿勢を継続
―6・7月の油価下落等を背景に素材業種の仕入価格判断DIが低下。対して販売価格判断DIは横ばい。コスト低下局面で
も販売価格を維持しており、企業利益の押し上げ要因に
 企業の中長期的な物価見通しも2％台半ばと、日銀の物価目標を上回る水準で高止まり
― 足元の原油価格再上昇やタンカー運賃高騰を受け、仕入価格が再び上昇する可能性には留意。企業の価格設定姿勢
が一段と強まるリスクも
販売価格判断DI・仕入価格判断DI（大企業・製造業） 物価全般の見通し（全規模・全産業）

日本：利上げでも企業金融は安定。企業部門の金利耐性を確認
6
 資金繰り判断は総じて底堅い。これまでの利上げの影響は限定的
― 利上げの影響が懸念された中小企業の資金繰り判断は横ばい圏の推移。価格転嫁の進展などを受けた事業環境の改
善が資金繰りを支えている構図
― 金利上昇局面でも銀行貸出は増加基調を維持しており、企業の資金需要も底堅く推移
 企業収益や資金繰りが金利上昇への耐性を示す中、金融環境はなお緩和的
― 市場のターミナルレート予想は2.5％程度まで上昇し、日銀の中立金利推計レンジ上限に接近

（%）
欧州：G7は原油・ディーゼルの協調放出で合意。米国の輸出制限リスクは後退
7
 G7はIEAを通じ4カ月で1億バレルの石油備蓄放出に合意。初期20日間はディーゼルを前倒し放出
― 背景にディーゼル価格高騰。中間選挙を控えたトランプ大統領は、禁輸も辞さない姿勢を強調（米国は世界需要の4.9％
を供給）。米国からの輸出が欧州の需要を支える一方、米国よりも潤沢な在庫を保有する欧州に圧力をかけた格好
― 欧州は石油製品消費に占めるディーゼル比率が高く、価格高騰を回避する思惑も協調放出の合意を後押し
― 合意ではG7域内でのエネルギー輸出制限回避も確認され、米国によるEU向け禁輸リスクはひとまず後退
石油製品の製品別消費割合の国際比較
（出所）Energy Institute より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成
EUの石油製品消費はディーゼル・軽油が中心

### Page 9

欧州：ディーゼル需給逼迫は残存。欧州のインフレ上振れ要因に
8
 ロシアのディーゼル輸出は足元で急減。中東からの輸出減、中国の輸出停止も重なり、世界需要の約6％が失われた格好
―G7の1億バレル放出で当面の供給不安は緩和するものの、構造的な供給不足の解消には至らず
―IEAも、中東からの原油輸出は回復する一方、石油製品の供給制約はなお深刻と指摘。ディーゼル需給の逼迫が続け
ば、輸送・物流コストを通じたインフレ圧力が残存
 欧州では9月HICPが再加速。ディーゼル高が輸送・物流コストを通じて追加的なインフレ上振れ要因となるリスク
―9月消費者物価（HICP）は前年比＋3.8％（前月：同＋3.2％）と伸び加速。エネルギー価格の上昇が主因
今般の備蓄放出量と停止中のディーゼル輸出
（注） 世界需要およびロシア・中国の輸出量は2025年実績。中東は2026年8月実績を参
考に、輸出量を前年の4分の1としてシェアを概算
（出所）EIA、IEA、OPEC、各種報道より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

注目点②G7備蓄放出でもくすぶるディーゼル供給不安

米国：雇用は減速も、AI関連需要が下支え
 9月の非農業部門雇用者数は前月差＋2.9万人と市場予想（同＋9.0万人）を下回る結果。7月・8月も下方修正
― 民間部門が前月差＋4.6万人（8月同+8.9万人）と増勢が鈍化したほか、政府部門が同▲1.7万人と再びマイナス転化
― 民間部門では、旺盛なAI投資需要などを背景に、製造や建設、建設・エンジニアリングなどが増加基調を維持したもの
の、情報、金融、専門サービスなどの高賃金セクターが減少
― 時間当たり賃金の伸びは前月比＋0.1％（8月同＋0.3％）と鈍化。こちらも市場予想（同＋0.3％）を下回る結果
― 総じて冴えない結果ながら、3カ月平均では＋5万人程度を維持しており、雇用は減速しつつも失速には至らず

9
（出所） 米労働省より、みずほ総合研究所調査部作成

米国：対人型業種の雇用に底堅さ。労働参加率上昇で失業率は小幅上昇
 対人接触度の高い業種では雇用に底堅さ、AIによる代替が進みにくい可能性
― ヘルスケアや建設、飲食サービスなど、対人接触機会の多い業種では雇用が増加する一方、経理・会計サービス、ITシ
ステム開発などでは低調
 失業率は4.2％と小幅悪化も引き続き低水準を維持
― 主に16-24歳の若年層およびプライムエイジ層の労働参加率の上昇が押し上げに寄与した模様

中国：景況感は急速に改善。建設業が持ち直し。政府は住宅ローンの利子補給で市場テコ入れ
 9月の総合PMIは50.7と、前月（49.5）から大幅に上昇。景況感が急速に改善。建設業が大きく持ち直し
― 製造業PMIが50.1（前月：49.8）、非製造業PMIが50.2（前月：49.0）とともに節目回復。製造業の生産（51.7）が堅調
― 建設業PMIは50.3と、前月（46.9）から急上昇。異常気象の影響はく落と財政予算執行の本格化があるとみられる
 中国政府が9/28、新規住宅ローン金利の年率1％分を補助する政策を発表。低迷が続く不動産市場をテコ入れ
― 対象物件の購入金額を150万元（3,500万円）以下に限定し、低中所得者層の住宅ローン負担を軽減する
― これにより、余剰在庫に苦しむ地方都市での実需を掘り起こし、市況のさらなる悪化に歯止めをかけたい考え

### Page 13

 中国のASEAN向け輸出品は、ASEANの製造品と競合。競合分野は一般機械や玩具、食品など幅広に
― 一般機械等に加え、ベトナムは電子機器や紙・木・繊維等の軽工業品、インドネシアは輸送機械でも競合度が高い
 中国製品流入が強まる中、ASEANはアンチダンピング、セーフガード措置に加え、越境ECへの規制強化などで対応
― 素材・中間財についてはアンチダンピング措置で対応。最終財では、低価格輸入品への課税や価格規制で応じる方向
― 中国企業等による規制回避を懸念し、ASEAN側が対象品目拡張や反迂回措置を強化する動きもあるが、こうした措置
の実効性は不透明
アジア：中国製品との競争激化でASEANの防衛策が拡大

 2026年以降に相違が目立つ台湾と韓国におけるAI需要の恩恵

日本：コアCPIは政策補助効果のはく落を主因に加速。その背後で徐々に高まるインフレ圧力
14
 9月の都区部コアCPIは前年比＋2.7％（前月：同＋1.8％）と大幅加速。政策要因のはく落に加え、価格転嫁の影響も拡大
― 東京都で実施された水道基本料金無償化・保育所保育料無償化の影響がはく落し、全体の伸びを＋0.56％Pt押し上げ

## Document 056: 2026年9月美国CPI数据点评：美国9月CPI：风险在油价的Higher for Longer

Source PDF: all report pdf/2026年9月美国CPI数据点评：美国9月CPI：风险在油价的Higher for Longer.pdf
Report source: 东吴证券
Date: 2026-10-15
Pages: 7
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

证券研究报告·宏观报告·宏观点评

东吴证券研究所

1 / 7 
请务必阅读正文之后的免责声明部分 
宏观点评 20261015  
美国 9 月 CPI：风险在 油价的 Higher for 
Longer——2026 年 9 月美国 CPI 数据点评

2026 年 10 月 15 日

相关研究  
《A 股 9 月复盘与 10 月展望——缩
量调整中的结构性分化，与政策托底
的十字路口》 
2026-10-14 
《 9 月美国 CPI 前瞻： 核心环比预计
温和，仍需关注油价扰动 ——海外周
报 20261011》 
2026-10-11

[Table_Tag]    [Table_Summary] 
◼ 核心观点：9 月美国 CPI 全面超预期，核心项目是超预期的主要来源。
与 8 月少数项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与服务环比的反弹具有广泛
性，通胀扩散度显著走高，超级核心通胀 3 个月均年率升至 4%以上的
高位，且二手车、 房租等分项近期走势与领先指数 “脱钩” 。这一通胀黏
性背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性超预期，充分条件则是油价
“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的传导。展望看，若油价持续在
高位反复， 则高油价给明年通胀带来的基数效应利好的意义将减弱。货
币政策方面，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否对 11 月 3 日的美国中期选举
尤其重要， 因此虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高， 但我们更担忧伊朗在 10
月下旬升级冲突、给特朗普施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息
预期走高的风险。就策略而言，中期选举仍是一个重要的事件节点，中
选后各方暂时消停、 美国国会格局清晰，市场的不确定性才会消除；我
们维持此前观点：10 月的不确定性高， 对风险偏好构成持续压制， 更适
合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 美国 9 月 CPI：全面超预期， 加息预期升温。美国 9 月 CPI 环比+0.65%，
预期+0.6%， 前值+0.4%； 核心CPI 环比+0.33%， 预期+0.2%， 前值+0.29%。
CPI 同比+3.74%，预期+3.6%，前值+3.40%；核心 CPI 同比+2.56%，预
期+2.5%，前值+2.45%。由于总体 CPI 环比基本符合预期，核心项目的
超预期是 9 月通胀全面超预期的主要驱动，且核心 CPI 环比是在 8 月
“异常”的高基数上延续高于预期，带动核心 CPI 环比 3 个月均值显
著走高。 数据发布后，紧货币交易强化，交易员加息定价10 月 28 日美
联储加息概率从 20%左右升至最高 50%以上，全年加息预期最高升至约
1.5 次，美股和黄金下跌，美债收益率和美元上涨。随后，数位美联储
官员发言仍释放鸽派信号，表示不急于加息，10 月加息预期回落至约
30%，美债利率回落。 
◼ 通胀结构：核心分项环比全面走高，通胀广度扩散。总体来看，核心商
品、 居住服务、 除居住核心服务等核心CPI 大类在 9 月均环比反弹， 显
示通胀黏性仍大。①核心商品方面，9 月核心商品 CPI 环比+0.25%，为
2025 年 2 月以来最高， 前值+0.11%；结构上， 全球供应链压力、 关税和
AI 相关商品涨价均是贡献来源。其中，高占比的二手车价格拉动 9.6bps，
是核心商品环比上涨的主要贡献，且与其领先指标 Manheim 二手车走
势脱钩。与关税相关的休闲商品与服装， 二者环比分别由上月的+0.02%、
0%反弹至+0.31%、+0.86%，其中休闲商品中的玩具环比涨幅突出，拉
动核心 CPI 环比 6.6bps。②居住通胀方面，9 月居住通胀环比+0.39%，
较前值 +0.26%显著 反弹， 其 中租 金和 自住 房折算 环 比分别 反弹 至
+0.25%、+0.35%的较高中枢， 酒店住宿环比保持在+2.04%的高位。从领
先指标——领先约 16 个月的房价和领先约 6—8 个月的租金来看，居住
通胀应当仍有一定的下行空间，其在本月的反弹略超预期。③超级核心
通胀方面，由于上月的电信服务等一次性扰动的消退，9 月非居住核心
服务通胀环比由前值+0.42%回落至+0.34%， 超级核心通胀 （包括居住服
务中的酒店住宿） 环比由前值+0.51%回落至+0.42%，但环比均处于较高
中枢，且 3 个月均年率分别升至 4.43%、4.48%的高位。尤其是，从结
构来看，交运、休闲服务、酒店等更广泛的项目出现了上涨，超级核心
通胀结构所体现的通胀黏性更加突出。④通胀扩散度方面，反映通胀广
度的通胀扩散指数在 9 月显著走高，环比折年增速大于过去 5 年均值的
项目占比从 17.6%提升至 24.76%，环比折年增速大于 4%的项目占比从
20%提升至 35%。 
◼ 展望与策略启示： 风险在于油价的“higher for longer”：总体来看，9 月
美国 CPI 全面超预期， 其中核心项目是超预期的主要来源。 与8 月少数
项目的扰动相比，9 月核心商品与核心服务环比的反弹具有广泛性，通
胀扩散度显著走高， 我们认为其背后的必要条件是美国经济增长的韧性

### Page 2

2 / 7 
超预期，充分条件则是油价“Higher for Longer”的现实强化了通胀的
传导， 这对美联储来说是一个不容乐观的信号。展望看，①通胀趋势方
面，若油价持续在高位反复，则我们此前看好的“高油价带来的基数效
应”意义减弱：一来是油价的回落幅度有限， 产生的基数效应更加有限；
二来是高油价的持续意味着通胀更大的扩散风险。②货币政策方面，对
于特朗普而言，10 月 28 日美联储加息与否，对 11 月 3 日的美国中期
选举尤其重要，因此，虽然地缘事件的不确定性很高，但我们更担忧上
行风险，即伊朗在中期选举前的 10 月下旬主动挑高油价、给特朗普政
府施压的可能性，这将强化美联储 10 月加息预期走高的风险。就策略
启示而言， 我们强调在此前报告中的观点， 美国中期选举仍然是一个重
要的事件节点，中选后美伊双方暂时消停、美国国会格局清晰， 市场的
不确定性才会逐步消除；10 月的不确定性高，对风险偏好构成持续压
制，因此更适合 11 月 3 日中选结果公布后进行右侧交易。 
◼ 风险提示：油价失控推高通胀； 美联储维持高利率水平时间过长， 引发
金融系统流动性危机。

东吴证券投资评级标准 
投资评级基于分析师对报告发布日后 6 至 12 个月内行业或公司回报潜力相对基准表现
的预期（A 股市场基准为沪深 300 指数，香港市场基准为恒生指数，美国市场基准为标普 
500 指数， 新三板基准指数为三板成指 （针对协议转让标的） 或三板做市指数 （针对做市转
让标的），北交所基准指数为北证 50 指数），具体如下： 
公司投资评级： 
买入：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在 15%以上； 
增持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于 5%与 15%之间； 
中性：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-5%与 5%之间； 
减持：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准介于-15%与-5%之间； 
卖出：预期未来 6 个月个股涨跌幅相对基准在-15%以下。 
行业投资评级： 
增持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对强于基准 5%以上； 
中性： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对基准-5%与 5%； 
减持： 预期未来 6 个月内，行业指数相对弱于基准 5%以上。 
我们在此提醒您， 不同证券研究机构采用不同的评级术语及评级标准。 我们采用的是相
对评级体系， 表示投资的相对比重建议。 投资者买入或者卖出证券的决定应当充分考虑自身
特定状况， 如具体投资目的、 财务状况以及特定需求等， 并完整理解和使用本报告内容， 不
应视本报告为做出投资决策的唯一因素。

## Document 068: 261005_daiwa_GUC

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_GUC.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: Global Unichip (GUC) reported September revenue which closed its 3Q26 top-
line 19% above market expectations thanks to its stronger-than-expected turnkey revenue 
run-rate during the quarter, likely attributable to more capacity support from its key foundry 
helping GUC meet strong demand from customers for AI projects. We see upside risk to 
our/consensus 3Q26 bottom-line estimates but are currently comfortable with our 4Q26 
forecasts despite the 3Q26 revenue beat likely dwarfing QoQ growth for 4Q26. We have a 
Buy (1) rating on the counter and will review our model in due course post its quarterly 
results release.

Highlight 
• 3Q26 top-line beating expectations. GUC released September revenue of TWD7.1bn, 
up 22% MoM (+98% YoY, see chart below), which closed its 3Q26 top-line at 
TWD18.8bn, up 35% QoQ (+118% YoY) for a new record. 3Q26 revenue beat our and 
consensus estimates both by 19% each thanks to its turnkey business (ie, mass-
production) which contributed TWD16.7bn for 89% of total and beat our estimate by 
22%. We attribute the beat to likely its key foundry continuously unleashing extra 
capacity helping GUC meet the strong customer AI demand during the quarter.

• Quarterly results preview and outlook. We expect GUC to have earned 3Q26 net 
profit of TWD1.7bn, varying not much from the consensus estimate, yet given the top-
line beat, we now see upside risk to the market expectations. As for 4Q26, our top-line 
and bottom-line forecasts are currently 10% and 26% above the consensus, 
respectively, making us comfortable with our current forecasts since we see the 3Q26 
beat being driven by increased foundry capacity allocation for some shipment pull-in, 
thus dwarfing 4Q26’s QoQ growth on intact AI demand. As flagged in our previous note 
(Upgrading: unleashed capacity adding to growth, 5 August 2026), we continue to 
expect GUC to be a key beneficiary from the strong AI ASIC ramps, including AI CPU 
and accelerators.

Recommendation 
We rate GUC Buy (1) with a 12M TP of TWD 5,125 on our 4-quarter forward PER target of 
63x. The stock performed well recently which we see as largely pricing in the 3Q26 top-line 
beat, therefore only suggesting investors accumulate only on any technical pullback. Key 
downside risks are less foundry support and margin execution.

## Document 069: 261005_daiwa_TW-CCL

Source PDF: all report pdf/261005_daiwa_TW-CCL.pdf
Report source: line報告備份
Date: 2026-10-05
Pages: 5
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

Summary: After market hours on 5 October 2026, EMC, TUC, and ITEQ released their 
monthly sales results. Our read-through is positive as the three key CCL vendors beat 
sales expectations by 5-15% on better pricing and on-going spec upgrades. Our thesis is 
that the strong AI demand is causing a spill-over impact on the whole CCL industry with 
low-end CCL having c.100% price hike in 2026E and high-end CCL seeing a 40-50% price 
hike due to cost inflation in 2026E. Recent data points showed this thesis remains valid. 
Looking ahead, we expect high-end/low-end CCL to see additional c.10%/20% ASP 
increases in 4Q26E, which could be the key share price catalyst. We believe that recent 
negatives news (including the impact of PTFE and CPO/NPO on the industry) are 
overdone. The impact on high-end CCL from these new technologies is likely to be limited 
from the point of view of Taiwan CCL companies. As such, we remain positive that the 
three names in our CCL coverage will ride on the decent price hike trend given favourable 
supply-demand dynamics.

• EMC (2383 TT, TWD5,700, Buy [1]) 
EMC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD21.5bn (+7% MoM, +169% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD60.9bn; +29% QoQ, +142% YoY) reaching 112% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD54.4bn) and 110% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD55.5bn). We attribute 
the revenue strength to the higher-than-expected price hike and on-going spec upgrades 
(leading to higher ASPs). Management attributes c.50% of the MoM increase in revenue in 
September to the price hike and the other 50% came to product mix optimisation. Looking 
ahead, the company doesn’t rule out the potential to increase its ASP again in 4Q26 (after 
the first time in 2Q26) in order to reflect rising raw material prices since 2Q26. As such, we 
expect revenue to grow at least high teens again on the potential price head as well as 
ongoing spec upgrades.

EMC sees limited impact from PTFE and HC glass-fibre-free CCL. Based on our 
understanding, PTFE is still under certification accounting for only limited layers inside 
specified high-end PCB as of now. As such, its impact on the high-end CCL TAM should be 
limited. As for HC glass-fibre-free CCL, the rigidity of PCB using this type of CCL could be 
a key concern to us.

Lastly, regarding the recent Co-Packaged Optics/Near-Packaged Optics (CPO/NPO) 
news’s negative impact on the CCL TAM, we also believe it to be limited given the likely 
adoption date for CPO of 2030, based on EMC’s forecast. Besides, we believe the PCB 
board inside CPO scale-up switches at that time would still need to support an 800G/1.6T 
transmission speed (or even lower) which would still require high-end PCB transmission 
although the highest transmission bandwidth can increase to 3.2T via optics. Lastly, EMC 
believes the spec of CCL won’t downgrade to M4 in the worst-case scenario but would be 
at least M6. Last but not least, we believe this kind of CPO-connected AI server might only

• TUC (6274 TT, TWD1,780, Buy [1]) 
TUC’s September 2026 revenue was TWD6.1bn (+4% MoM, +115% YoY), which led to its 
3Q26 sales (TWD17.9bn; +26% QoQ, +123% YoY) reaching 103% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD17.4bn) and 105% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD17.1bn). Looking 
ahead, we expect TUC to deliver at least QoQ growth in 4Q26 given 1) a price hike along 
with its peers for high-end CCL (eg. M8) and 2) some shipment delay from 3Q26 given 
flooding in Southeast Asia. For the longer term, the company is promising a continual spec 
upgrade trend in 2027 with new projects using M9-grade CCL (including low dk1, low dk2, 
and Q-glass version) in 2027 for high-end switches, AI servers, and optical transceivers. 
Considering the insufficient capacity in the CCL industry, we expect TUC to further optimise 
its product mix after acquiring more high-end CCL orders in future quarters (vs. a single 
major AI server client in 2026). We have a Buy (1) rating on TUC, with a 12-month TP of 
TWD2,320, based on a target PER of 35x applied to our 1-year forward EPS forecast. Key 
downside risks: 1) weaker-than-expected demand for switches/AI servers, and 2) market 
share loss to CCL peers.

• ITEQ (6213 TT, TWD751, Buy [1]) 
ITEQ’s September 2026 revenue was TWD5.8bn (+15% MoM, +111% YoY), which led to 
its 3Q26 sales (TWD15.6bn; +35% QoQ, +102% YoY) reaching 114% of our 3Q26 forecast 
(TWD13.7bn) and 108% of the Bloomberg consensus forecast (TWD14.5bn). The number 
for 3Q26 was higher than our estimate due likely to a stronger-than-expected price hike 
(c.30% in 3Q26 vs. expectation of 20%). Looking ahead to 4Q26, we expect the company 
to further raise its pricing by 20-30% to factor in cost inflation in raw materials (E-glass) 
and the supply-demand gap. The company also expects the rising supply of e-glass (which 
could lift its utilisation rate from the current level of 75%) to lift its gross margin more 
significantly. For 2027, the company is excited about the new CPU adoption driving rising 
M7 CCL usage and is working on M9 certification with new end customers, which could 
become a key growth driver in 2028. We have a Buy (1) rating on ITEQ. Key downside 
risk: weaker-than-expected regular server demand.

ab 8 October 2026
Global Research
Initiation of Coverage
WinWay Technology
Final point of contact: securing AI yield through
advanced testing socket
Solid growth ahead with robust capacity expansions
We initiate coverage of AI/HPC test socket leader WinWay with a Buy rating and NT
$8,900 price target. WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak
due to broader market de-rating and margin pressure associated with its Technoprobe

partnership and capacity expansion. We believe the recent correction presents an
opportunity to re-engage with WinWay's long-term growth trajectory, supported by
three key drivers: 1) increasing content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) broader adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together,
these drivers should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.
Importantly, WinWay's growth is increasingly being driven by higher socket content per

device rather than semiconductor unit growth alone and the stock currently trades at
16x of our 2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x, potential to re-rate with GM recovery
in the coming quarters. See our companion testing deep dive sector note.
Rising yields and utilisation to offset probe card dilution
Gross margin declined to 38.3% in Q226 from 45.3% in FY25, pressured by outsourced
MEMS probes and expansion-related costs. While probe-card margins are unlikely to

return to H125 levels of 48.9% given the Technoprobe partnership structure, we expect
margin pressure to ease as utilization and yields improve, supporting GM recovery to
43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in 2028E.
CPU and TPU as fundamental growth drivers
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise,  WinWay is well positioned to benefit from the
expansion of AI-oriented CPU and TPU platforms. We expect the company to supply
sockets for AMD's Venice CPU, Nvidia's Vera CPU, and Google's TPU v8t and v9, and

also Vera CPU probe cards, providing an additional growth engine beyond GPUs.
WinWay is well positioned in this segment due to its historical collaboration with end
customers and its proximity to the local Taiwanese test supply chain. We view AI CPUs
and TPUs as an increasingly important contributor to test interface demand as compute
architectures diversify in the agentic AI era, driving WinWay's socket and probe card
sales growth of 132%/75%/69% and 94%/72%/78% in 2026E/27E/28E, respectively.

We estimate CPU/TPU to contribute  around 30%/20% of WinWay's 2027E sales.
Valuation: Initiate with a Buy rating, with price target of NT$8,900
We forecast EPS of NT$101.8 in 2026E, rising to NT$201.4/393.0 in 2027E/28E. Our NT
$8,900 price target is based on 30x average 2027E-28E P/E, in line with the company's
historical average. We believe this valuation is supported by WinWay's exposure to
structural growth in AI/HPC testing, expanding socket content, and our long-term

earnings CAGR estimate of 30%. We initiate with a Buy rating.

Q: Will capacity constraints limit WinWay's growth in the next two to three years?
Near term, yes. Beyond 2026E, no. WinWay's current capacity remains constrained, but ongoing
expansions should significantly increase output over the next two years. With new capacity coming

online and the company shifting toward higher-value AI/HPC applications, including CPU probe
cards, >10k-pin coaxial sockets and next-generation HyperSockets, we forecast revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E, supported by capacity additions, rising AI/HPC content and
expanding socket demand. We also expect operating leverage to improve as capacity scales and
utilization increases, with OPM rising from 27.5% in 2026E to 32.1%/36.7% in 2027E/28E.

Q: Will WinWay's GM recover from Q226's 38.3%?
Yes. We view recent margin pressure as largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe
sourcing through Technoprobe and start-up costs associated with capacity expansion. Although
margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel due to the MEMS partnership terms
with Technoprobe, as new facilities ramp and yields improve, we expect profitability to recover
gradually from the Q226 trough, with gross margin improving to 43.1% in 2027E and 44.7% in

2028E.
We initiate coverage of WinWay with a Buy rating and NT$8,900 price target. We believe the market
is overly focused on near-term margin pressure and underappreciating WinWay's long-term growth
outlook. Key drivers include 1) expanding content from AI GPUs, CPUs, and TPUs, 2) substantial
capacity expansion, and 3) growing adoption of system-level testing (SLT). Together, these drivers
should support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E.

TSMC and OSATs have been expanding their CoWoS capacities aggressively, from 40kwpm in 2024
to around 90 kwpm in 2025, and is tracking to expand CoWoS capacity toward 160kwpm  in 2026E.
TSMC also ramped up expanding N3/N2 capacity, with multiple product launches from customers'
pipelines—Nvidia's Rubin, AMD's MI400 series/Venice CPU and other cloud ASICs—translating into
foreseeable, strong testing demand as the majority are WinWay's customers. AI accelerator test times

have increased meaningfully for new platforms. We expect continued front-end and advanced
packaging investments to drive higher testing intensity and support long-term demand for advanced
sockets and test interfaces.
WinWay's share price has corrected 43% from its April 2026 peak and currently trades at 16x of our
2028E P/E vs historical average of 30x. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market
price implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term GM

level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%. We see scope for re-rating.

Testing TAM expanding with IC complexity: leaders likely to
outperform industry growth
Our central view is that testing content per AI accelerator is growing faster than
semiconductor unit volumes, creating a multi-year expansion in testing TAM. We believe
semiconductor testing is entering a period of structural growth as AI-driven chip
complexity increases testing intensity across wafer, package and system levels. The

industry's shift-left strategy, which moves more testing upstream in the semiconductor
manufacturing process, is resulting in longer and more frequent test insertions. As cloud
AI GPUs and ASICs become increasingly complex, additional testing is required to ensure
functionality, performance and yield, while reducing defective parts per million (DPPM).
More robust testing should improve deployment efficiency and lower the cost of
operating AI infrastructure.

Semiconductor testing generally comprises four stages: wafer/chip probing (CP), final
test (FT), system-level test (SLT), and burn-in. CP is conducted at the wafer-level before
packaging, while FT, SLT, and burn-in occur after packaging. The below Figure 1S e m i c o n d u c t o r  t e s t i n g  p r o c e s s :  f r o m  w a f e r  l e v e l  t o  p a c k a g e  l e v e l
illustrates the semiconductor manufacturing flow.
Figure 1: Semiconductor testing process: from wafer level to package level

Source: UBS
We estimate semiconductor testing historically accounts for ~1% of total
semiconductor revenue. Within testing, consumables such as probe cards, sockets and
interface boards represent ~10-15% of industry revenue. According to Yole, the probe
card, socket and interface board markets should grow from US$3.8bn, US$2.3bn and
US$1.7bn in 2026 to US$5.5bn, US$3.1bn and US$2.0bn by 2031, respectively.

WinWay is expanding capacity rapidly to capture this growing TAM. Industry test
interface suppliers are expanding capacity aggressively to meet rising AI and HPC
demand. As advanced semiconductors require higher-performance probe cards, sockets
and interface boards, capacity additions are becoming increasingly important to support

growth across both wafer-level and package-level testing.
We believe WinWay is well positioned to gain share within the high-end AI/HPC test
interface market. Taiwanese suppliers benefit from their proximity to leading fabless
customers and the growing preference for localised supply chains, particularly for
advanced testing applications where responsiveness and engineering support are
critical. Against a backdrop of tight industry supply, WinWay announced significant

capacity expansion plans over the next two to three years.
WinWay's capacity is expected by the company to increase from 3.5mn pins per month
at year-end 2025 to 6mn currently, reaching 9mn by year-end 2026 and 14mn by year-
end 2027 through expansions in Renwu, Kaohsiung. By 2027, approximately 60% of
pogo-pin requirements should be internally sourced, strengthening both supply security
and margins.
We estimate these capacity additions will support revenue growth of 105%/74%/71%

in 2026E/27E/28E.

For WinWay, this growth opportunity is substantial, as the capacity expansions for its

could contribute around NT$123 of EPS in 2027E, rising to NT$261 in 2028E. Combined
with its expanding capacity, strong customer relationships and proximity to Taiwan's
semiconductor testing ecosystem, including ASE/SPIL, KYEC and major handler
suppliers, WinWay is well positioned to gain share in the AI/HPC socket market.

### Page 6

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 6
The AI era has materially increased the value of semiconductor testing. Compared with
traditional mobile and automotive chips, AI accelerators incorporate larger compute
dies, more advanced nodes, chiplet architectures, advanced packaging technologies,
multiple I/O dies, and increasing HBM content. These factors drive higher-value test
consumables, including advanced MEMS probe cards, multilayer interface boards, and

next-generation sockets designed to support higher pin counts, thermal loading, and
power delivery requirements.
Capacity expansion by test interface vendors is ultimately supported by continued
investment throughout the advanced packaging ecosystem, from providers such as
TSMC, ASE, Intel, and Amkor. During the Q226 earnings calls, TSMC raised its recent
2026 capex from US$52-56bn to US$60-64bn and ASE raised its 2026 capex from US
$8.5bn to US$10.5bn, with both companies citing robust demand supporting capex

raise, we believe capex for the next two years will be even higher than 2026, indicating
continuous growth in future years. With advanced packaging technologies getting
significantly more complex and with larger form factor, illustrated by the upcoming
ramp of both TSMC's CoPoS (chip-on-panel-on-substrate) and Intel's EMIB-T
(embedded multi-die interconnect bridge) packaging technologies, test interface
solution providers will benefit with more value added consumables (see our CoPoS vs

EMIB-T deep dive).

New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC applications, are set to increase
in both front-end wafer fabrication advancements and back-end packaging
complexities with each iterations (i.e. Nvidia's roadmap from Blackwell to Rubin to

Feynman or Google's TPU roadmap from v7 to v8 to v9).   This trend is evident with
TSMC's front-end  and TSMC, Intel, and OSAT's back-end advanced packaging capacity
expansions. For front-end, we see industry N3 capacity expanding from 156kwpm in
year end 2025 to 224/283/293kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E, and N2 capacity from
47kwpm in year-end 2025 to 111/200/312kpwm in year end 2026E/27E/28E. For back-
end, we are seeing industry CoWoS capacity to rise from 90kwpm in 2025 to 160kwpm

in 2026E and further expand to 270kwpm in 2027E.

Final-test, system-level test, and burn-in content should continue to increase as AI

accelerators become larger, more power-hungry and more complex. Rising thermal
design power and packaging complexity increase validation requirements, driving

### Page 7

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 7
longer test times and higher demand for test equipment to prevent testing from
becoming a production bottleneck. This is where WinWay's sockets comes into play—
as high-end chips with 2.5D/3D/3.5D packaging requires intricate FT sockets to handle
the high thermal design power (TDP) and high data rates of up to 224G during the FT
process for reliable test results.
Figure 10: Our FT test time estimates

While architectural changes can affect test requirements for individual product
generations, we expect the long-term trend of rising testing content to remain intact.
For example, Nvidia's Rubin Ultra adopts a simpler package structure than previously
expected, from a 4-die structure to a 2-die structure and Rubin Ultra HBM de-spec from
HBM4E 12-Hi to initially use HBM4 8-Hi and upgrade to HBM4E 8-Hi at the latter stages,

which may moderate near-term test growth. However, future products such as Nvidia's
Feynman are expected to reintroduce greater package complexity, with a four-GPU per
package structure. More importantly, any reduction in test time can be offset by higher
production volumes, making throughput requirements equally important for testing
demand.
We expect test interface content growth to outpace unit growth, For instance, test
socket's ASP directly correlates with its pin counts, with the addition of 20-30% more

pins inside the sockets, socket ASP can grow 20-30% or more as the socket might
require significant cooling capabilities as the TDP of the DUT rises with its larger form
factor. Unlike prior semiconductor cycles, much of the current demand is driven by rising
test interface content per device rather than unit growth alone.
Robust growth drivers across semiconductor testing
We believe WinWay is set to benefit from several semiconductor testing growth drivers

and capture the AI induced, expanding testing TAM. We see three main growth drivers
for WinWay: 1) AI accelerators (GPU + TPU), 2) agentic AI driven CPU demand, and 3)
shift-left testing trend. Below we list the three main growth drivers in detail.
Accelerators still in focus with TPU as another significant growth driver
Nvidia has been the primary driver of testing content growth over the past several years,
benefiting the entire semiconductor testing supply chain. We expect this trend to

continue as GPU architectures evolve from Blackwell to Rubin and then to Feynman,
with increasing process node complexity, larger package sizes and more advanced
packaging technologies driving higher testing value per device.
We believe Nvidia should remain a major contributor to  WinWay, with GPUs continuing
to serve as their core growth engine. We estimate WinWay's socket share could improve
from a roughly 50/50 split with Smiths Interconnect in prior generations to 60/40 with

Rubin. Nvidia currently accounts for approximately 50% of WinWay's revenue. Both
Nvidia GPUs and Google TPUs increase testing demand through higher content per
device rather than unit growth alone.

### Page 8

WinWay Technology 8 October 2026 ab 8
Beyond Nvidia, Google's TPU roadmap represents a significant incremental growth
opportunity.  In our view, WinWay should benefit as the socket supplier for both TPU v8t
and the next-generation TPU v9 platform. We believe MediaTek's increasing share gain
for Google TPUs will also benefit the local Taiwanese test interface supply chain. We
estimate TPU to contribute to around 20% of WinWay 2027E sales.
We view TPU v9 as a particularly attractive opportunity given its large four-die package

architecture based on Intel's EMIB-T packaging technology, which should drive higher
testing content. We estimate production of 4mn TPU v8t units in 2027E, followed by
2.3mn TPU v9 packages (9.2mn dies) and an additional 2mn TPU v8t units in 2028E.
Importantly, while EMIB-T packaging is expected to be performed in Malaysia, we
believe the majority of advanced testing value should remain within the Taiwanese
testing ecosystem. Leading testing providers, including ASE and KYEC, maintain

substantial testing operations across Taiwan and Southeast Asia, supporting continued
participation by Taiwanese test interface suppliers. See our latest MediaTek report and
EMIB-T deep dive.
CPU business as a supplemental catalyst for test interface companies in
the agentic world
We believe semiconductor test interface are an underappreciated beneficiary of the
emerging agentic AI ecosystem. UBS estimates the CPU market will expand to US
$170bn by 2030E from US$31bn in 2025 and US$71bn in 2027E, driven by rising

deployment of AI infrastructure. As CPU-to-GPU ratios increasingly approach 1:1 in AI
clusters, demand for testing interfaces should expand alongside CPU volumes. TSMC is
the main CP test provider in the industry, with probe card suppliers aiding its massive
demand by supplying probes, such as TSMC's GPU probe cards supported by
Technoprobe's probes. However, with capacity now becoming scarce with strong front
and back-end demand, TSMC is outsourcing its CP business to ASE and KYEC, further

broadening the testing supply chain. This is where new probe card vendors come in—
smaller probe card players in the past, such as  WinWay, now has the opportunity to gain
significant, non-GPU share from TSMC, as they have MEMS probe capabilities
(WinWay's MEMS probe are from its strategic partnership with Technoprobe) and has a
long established relationship with the fabless companies such as Nvidia via supplying
non-probe card consumables such as sockets. Of which, CPU projects has become the

main area for  WinWay to expand their footprint.
We expect WinWay to benefit from the ramp of Nvidia's ARM-based Vera CPU and
Google's ARM-based Axion CPU in 2026E-27E. WinWay is positioned to supply sockets
and probe cards for Vera and sockets for Axion. Additional upside could come from
AMD's Venice CPU, where WinWay is well positioned. For WinWay, CPU-related
demand could account for approximately 30% of sales in 2026E-27E. Importantly, CPU
programmes diversify exposure beyond AI GPUs and provide a second structural growth

driver for test interface suppliers.
Shift-left igniting secular growth for chip probing
The industry's adoption of shift-left testing should drive a multi-year structural growth
opportunity for chip probing (CP) and its supply chain. As advanced packaging costs rise
and AI accelerators become increasingly complex, semiconductor manufacturers are
placing greater emphasis on identifying known-good dies (KGD) before packaging. The
test shift-left trend will  drive structural AI/HPC probe card and test consumables

demand, with increasing test interface content as test insertions and test time increase.
WinWay should also benefit through its partnership with Technoprobe, with probe card
revenue growth remaining robust at 94%/72% in 2026E/27E following 161%/260%
growth in 2024/25.

The economics of advanced packaging reinforce this trend. Heterogeneous integration,
chiplet architectures and 2.5D/3D packaging significantly increase package value, while

defects identified late in the manufacturing process can lead to substantial scrap costs.
To mitigate this risk, foundries and OSATs such as TSMC and ASE employ distributed
testing flows incorporating KGD, known-good-stack (KGS) and assembly test screening,
to catch defects before the components enter a multi-week packaging cycle (>2 weeks
for CoW and 2 weeks for oS). The importance of these tests rises as AI clusters become
increasingly dependent on component reliability and low defective-parts-per-million

(DPPM) rates. Given the multiplicative yield degradation, a 300bps yield decline at just
one manufacturing stage (e.g. dropping from 99.5% to 96.5%) reduces overall
integrated four-stage yield from 98.0% to 95.0%; on a 500,000 unit ramp at US
$10,000 per unit, this 3% scrap delta translates into around US$150m in unrecoverable
losses given >6-month manufacturing lead times.
At the same time, rising chip power consumption is increasing the technical difficulty of

wafer-level testing. As chip thermal design power scales beyond 1,000W and power
density is expected by the industry to surge  another 8x by 2030, traditional wafer-level
chip probe (WLCP) encounters severe physical bottlenecks. Finer probe pitches and
higher power densities increase the risk of overheating, probe needle burnout and yield
degradation, creating demand for more advanced probing and thermal-control
solutions.

WinWay set to benefit from the AI trend with its leading
position within advanced test socket market

WinWay  was founded in 2001 in Kaohsiung, Taiwan, and is a leading provider of
semiconductor testing interface solutions, with test sockets representing its core
business. The company focuses on advanced AI/HPC applications, supplying sockets for

final test (FT), system-level test (SLT) and burn-in, as well as cobra probe cards  and
partnership with Technoprobe for MEMS probe cards.
WinWay's business is primarily divided into two segments:
 Test socket: Sockets are WinWay's largest revenue contributor. The company
supplies a broad range of testing sockets, including coaxial, PoP/plastic and burn-
in sockets, serving applications ranging from consumer electronics to high-
performance computing.

 Coaxial socket: utilised as the mainstream option for nearly all AI/HPC chips,
as traditional sockets are near its physical limits. Coaxial sockets need to handle
data rates of up to 224G for high speed transmission and high chip TDP during
the FT process.
 HyperSocket: a rubber-silicone like layer called elastomer are placed on top
of the pogo pins, these elastomer pins causes zero damage to the chips being
tested under high contact forces. WinWay's next generation HyperSocket

technology utilises elastomers to provide a softer contact between the DUT
and the pogo pins, avoiding damage to solder ball. This structure significantly
enhances spring probe's lifetime, reduces cleaning frequency, and achieves
maximum production efficiency. We estimate it will contribute around 5% of
sales in 2027E with a 20-30% ASP premium as compared to standard coaxial
socket.
 Probe cards:  WinWay historically supplies cobra probe cards (vertical probe

cards), but with the company announcing their five-year probe supply deal with
Technoprobe for MEMS probe in December 2025, they are now one of Nvida Vera
CPU's main probe card suppliers.
AI accelerators are pushing socket requirements beyond 10,000 pins, making coaxial
sockets the industry standard for leading-edge AI/HPC devices. These sockets must
support transmission speeds of up to 224G while handling significantly higher thermal
design power (TDP) during testing.

Elastomer technology is increasingly being adopted alongside spring-probe (pogo-pin)
architectures to reduce chip damage and improve contact reliability. WinWay's next-
generation HyperSocket integrates elastomer materials between the DUT and spring
probes, improving probe lifetime, reducing cleaning requirements and enhancing
production efficiency. We believe HyperSocket could become an important
differentiator as next-generation AI accelerators demand higher pin counts, bandwidth

and thermal performance. We estimate HyperSocket could contribute approximately
5% of WinWay's revenue in 2027E while carrying a 20-30% ASP premium versus
conventional coaxial sockets. The transition toward larger AI packages, higher pin
counts and faster data-transfer speeds should continue to increase socket content per
device. We expect this trend to support long-term growth in the socket market, with
technology leaders such as WinWay well positioned to gain share through next-

generation solutions including HyperSocket.
Beyond its existing product portfolio, WinWay is developing next-generation
technologies including CPO electrical test sockets and liquid-cooled sockets for future
high TDP AI accelerators, reinforcing its position within the AI/HPC testing ecosystem.

WinWay's competitive advantages are its 1) technology leadership with coaxial socket
and HyperSocket, 2) proximity to the semiconductor testing value chain in Taiwan, and
3) historical relationship with key AI companies such as Nvidia and AMD. We believe

these advantages may allow WinWay to capture significant upside in the growing
semiconductor testing TAM driven by cloud AI proliferation, and gain share across its
competition such as Smiths Interconnect for AI and HPC applications.

Compared with the probe card market, the socket industry is more fragmented.
Competition include:
 WinWay (6515.TW) : A major share gainer in AI/HPC sockets, with strong
exposure to Nvidia and AMD. The company specializes in high-performance
coaxial sockets and is developing its next-generation HyperSocket platform. It is

also expanding into probe cards through its partnership with Technoprobe.
 Leeno (058470.KQ) : Vertically integrated supplier focused on smartphone AP
and memory sockets.
 ISC (095340.KQ): Memory socket supplier with elastomer expertise.
 Smiths Interconnect (Acquired by Molex) : A key player in AI/HPC socket
industry, with its DaVinci coaxial sockets able to support high frequency
connectivity testing.
 Yamaichi (6941.T) : Japanese socket supplier with substantial automotive

exposure.
 Yokowo (6800.T): Major provider of contact probes and socket pins.
 Cohu (COHU.O) : Broad test-solutions provider spanning ATE, handlers and
sockets, with high exposure to automotive, industrial and consumer applications.

WinWay's margin is set to recover in 2027E/28E
Company GM hit a trough in Q226, at 38.3%. We view recent margin pressure as
largely temporary. GM has been impacted by MEMS probe sourcing through
Technoprobe (probe card GM now below 40% and dilutive to >50% coaxial socket GM)
and start-up costs associated with WinWay's rapid capacity expansion. During the early
part of 2025, WinWay's GM was high at 48.9% in H125, with more cobra probe cards

(60% GM) in mix with solid pogo pin and socket yield. With MEMS probe card now
accounting for more than 70% of probe card revenue and GM at around low to mid
30%, margin dilution from probe card business is unlikely to unravel for GM to return to
above 45% in the near term due to the MEMS partnership terms with Technoprobe.
However, we expect that as new facilities ramp and yields improve, profitability to
recover gradually from the Q226 trough. We see GM improving to 43.1% in 2027E and

44.7% in 2028E.

Appendix: What are semiconductor test sockets?
Semiconductor test socket are electro-mechanical interface that are used post chip
probing and chip packaging process to test the functionality of the chips. Sockets are
placed onto load boards on top of testers during the final test/system level test
processes. Handlers will place the chips onto the sockets, and pins inside the sockets will
enable the testers to read the signals from the device under test (DUT, i.e. the packaged

chips), with tester signals fan out  from beneath the PCB load board.
Figure 27: Socket illustration (with load
board underneath)
Source: UBS
As AI accelerators become larger and more complex, socket requirements continue to
increase. High-end sockets typically comprise a socket housing, alignment plate,
integrated thermal-control components and contact elements that connect the DUT to
the tester. Spring probes (pogo pins) are the most common choice for HPC sockets, with

high durability, individual probe replacement flexibility, and consistent contact force. FT
and SLT sockets account for more than 70% of the total socket market, with burn-in
sockets representing the remainder.

Peer Average = 25x -1std = 9x

Source: LSEG, UBS estimates
Despite a 120% YTD share-price increase, WinWay remains 43% below its April 2026
peak and trades at only 16x our 2028E P/E. We believe the market remains overly
focused on near-term GM pressure while underestimating the company's multi-year
growth opportunity. Our reverse DCF analysis indicates that the current market price
implies only a 19% long-term sales CAGR vs our estimate of 25%, alongside long-term
GM level of 32% vs our estimate of 45%.

We believe investors are not fully reflecting three key drivers:
1) CPU and TPU opportunities are becoming increasingly meaningful.
Beyond its core GPU socket franchise, WinWay is positioned to benefit from Nvidia's
Vera CPU, AMD's Venice CPU, and Google's TPU. These programmes should broaden
revenue sources and create an additional demand driver for high-end sockets and probe
cards.
2) Larger AI packages are driving higher socket content per device.

As AI accelerators adopt larger form factors, higher pin counts and greater power
consumption, socket complexity and ASPs continue to rise. We expect socket content
growth to outpace semiconductor unit growth, creating a structural tailwind for
WinWay.
3) Capacity ramp and margin recovery are not fully reflected.
Following substantial capacity expansion, we expect revenue growth of
105%/74%/71% in 2026E/27E/28E. At the same time, we forecast gross margin

recovery from 38.3% in Q226 to 43.1%/44.7% in 2027E/28E as utilization and yields
improve.
Our NT$8,900 price target is based on our valuation for WinWay at 30x average 2027E-
28E P/E, in-line with its historical average of 30x and underpinned by a 30% 2027-31E
earnings CAGR. Comparing with historical NTM peer average PE of roughly 25x
(currently trading at 39x NTM PE), we believe WinWay deserves to trade at a premium
given its leadership in AI/HPC sockets, expanding CPU and TPU exposure, and our long-

term earnings CAGR estimate.
WinWay is trading at NT$6,130 as of
6 Oct 2026

UPSIDE (NT$10,000): Our upside scenario reflects stronger than expected market share
gain among Nvidia's Rubin sockets and Vera CPU probe card strength, with strong

32.1%/36.7% in 2027/28E.
DOWNSIDE (NT$4,000):  Our downside scenario reflects slow GM recovery of

38.1%/39.7% in 2027/28E, with weaker growth for both socket and probe card
businesses for slower capacity ramp up, leading to YoY sales growth of 22.3%/34.8% in
2027/28E, respectively.

Company Description
Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a
semiconductor testing interface solution provider, with final test
sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-end
solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test
and burn-in test sockets and probe cards through its own-made
probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.

Industry Outlook
New iterations of semiconductors, especially for AI / HPC
applications, are set to increase in both front-end wafer
fabrication advancements and back-end packaging complexities
with each iterations. With the industry now moves to utilize
"shift left", chip probing and its relevant supply chain will be key
beneficiaries of this trend which we believe will unlock multi-
year, secular growth trends.

Company Description
WinWay was founded in 2001 in Kaoshiung, Taiwan. It is a semiconductor testing interface
solution provider, with final test sockets as its main business segment. The company has high-
end solutions for AI/HPC testing, providing final test/system-level test and burn-in test sockets

and probe cards through its own-made probes and a partnership with Technoprobe.
Valuation Method and Risk Statement
We value WinWay based on PE methodology.
Risks for the semis industry:
- Geopolitical uncertainties: Geopolitical uncertainties including tariffs and more export
control could negatively impact semis cycle, and a downturn would lead to slower demand in
the overall market and moderating capacity expansion.
- Meaningful deceleration of new technology development: Slower demand and less

investment are expected If there is meaningful deceleration of new technology development
such as Cloud AI, autonomous driving, etc.
- Rising utility cost: Larger-than-expected utility cost increases would further pressure
profitability. Rising utility costs in Taiwan have negatively impacted margins for Taiwan tech
companies.
Company risks:
- Faster- or slower-than-expected CoWoS capacity expansion: This could result in WinWay
gaining/losing socket/probe card orders from major end customers, foundries, and OSATs.

- Competitors achieving technological advancement: Potential risk of competition (from
either local or foreign companies) achieving technological advancements could result in
WinWay losing market share from major end customers, foundries and OSATs.
- Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth: Better- or worse-than-expected AI growth could
lead to a ramp-up or a slowdown of equipment orders from major foundries and OSATs to
WinWay, affecting long-term revenue growth potential.

- Slowdown or delay of new technological advancement: A potential slowdown or delay of
the rollout for technological advancements for technologies such as SoIC, CoPoS, and CPO,
would limit future socket/probe card orders.
- Increase of raw material costs: Rising raw material costs may directly impact WinWay’s
margins for socket/probe card shipments.

Investing in structured investments involves significant risks. For a detailed discussion of the risks involved in investing in any particular structured investment, you must read

the relevant offering materials for that investment. Structured investments are unsecured obligations of a particular issuer with returns linked to the performance of an
underlying asset. Depending on the terms of the investment, investors could lose all or a substantial portion of their investment based on the performance of the underlying
asset. Investors could also lose their entire investment if the issuer becomes insolvent. UBS does not guarantee in any way the obligations or the financial condition of any

issuer or the accuracy of any financial information provided by any issuer. Structured investments are not traditional investments and investing in a structured investment is
not equivalent to investing directly in the underlying asset. Structured investments may have limited or no liquidity, and investors should be prepared to hold their investment
to maturity. The return of structured investments may be limited by a maximum gain, participation rate or other feature. Structured investments may include call features and,

if a structured investment is called early, investors would not earn any further return and may not be able to reinvest in similar investments with similar terms. Structured
investments include costs and fees which are generally embedded in the price of the investment. The tax treatment of a structured investment may be complex and may differ
from a direct investment in the underlying asset. UBS and its employees do not provide tax advice. Investors should consult their own tax advisor about their own tax situation

before investing in any securities.
Important Information About Sustainable Investing Strategies: Sustainable investing strategies aim to consider and incorporate environmental, social and governance
(ESG) factors into investment process and portfolio construction. Strategies across geographies approach ESG analysis and incorporate the findings in a variety of ways.
Incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing considerations may inhibit the portfolio manager’s ability to participate in certain investment opportunities that otherwise

would be consistent with its investment objective and other principal investment strategies. The returns on a portfolio incorporating ESG factors or sustainable investing
considerations may be lower or higher than portfolios where ESG factors, exclusions, or other sustainability issues are not considered by the portfolio manager, and the
investment opportunities available to such portfolios may differ.

## Document 135: [329180] HD현대중공업(329180) HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트HD현대중공업(329180) HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트 - 기업

Source PDF: all report pdf/[329180] HD현대중공업(329180) HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트HD현대중공업(329180) HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트HD현대중공업 증설 공시 코멘트 - 기업.pdf
Report source: Hankyung
Date: 2026-09-11
Pages: 4
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

HD현대중공업은 전일(9/10) 대출력 발전엔진 (20MW, 9MW 급) 및 SMR 캐파 
확장을 위한 신규시설투자를 공시했다. 규모는 총 1조 722억원이며, 그 중 엔진 관련 
투자는 8,336억원, SMR 관련 투자는 2,386억원이다.

엔진 관련 투자에서는 4행정 중속엔진 캐파 확장을 위한 신규 공장 신설 및 기존 
공장 생산 설비 구축을 진행하고자 한다. 동사의 4행정 중속엔진 캐파는 기존 3GW 
규모이며, 이번 증설을 통해 신규 공장 캐파 3GW, 기존 공장 증설분 1.2GW가 
더해져 2028년 5월 기준 최종적으로 7.2GW 규모가 될 것으로 예상된다. 영암 
공장과 신축 공장에서는 데이터센터향으로 납품될 9MW 및 20MW 규모의 대출력 
엔진 위주, 기존 울산 공장은 3~5MW 규모의 선박용 엔진 위주의 생산이 이루어질 
계획이다. 이번 공장 신축 완료 이후에도 가용 부지가 남아있어 시장 수요에 따라 
향후 추가적인 캐파 확대 역시 고려할 수 있다.

SMR 투자의 경우 현대건설/테라파워와의 협력을 위한 SMR 주기기 연 2기 생산 
캐파 확보를 목적으로 진행되었다. 1기 당 출력 용량은 345MW이며, 구체적인 생산 
단가 및 물량의 경우 테라파워사와 지속 협의 중으로 동사의 미래 먹거리로서 
중장기적인 성장성에 기여할 전망이다.

데이터센터향 수요 성장에 대한 확신은 실제 엔진 업체들의 수주 인콰이어리 및 신규 
투자에서 드러나고 있다. 엔진 발전 시장 상위 업체 바르질라와 에버런스가 올해 
결정한 증설 규모는 총 4.5GW로 파악되며, 동사 역시 같은 행보로 신규 시장에서 
유의미한 점유율을 확보할 것으로 기대된다. 동사의 데이터센터향 4행정 중속엔진 
수주 단가를 고려했을 때 GW당 약 1조원의 매출 창출이 가능하다. 또한, 현재 동사 
엔진사업부의 캐시카우를 담당하고 있는 2행정 저속엔진 대비 좋은 수익성을 보여줄 
것으로 기대되는 바, 데이터센터향 생산이 시작될 28년 하반기부터 점진적으로 엔진 
믹스 개선 및 마진 성장이 가속화될 것으로 전망한다.

  4Q28 신공장 첫 엔진 생산 가능 예상 
2) 데이터센터향 엔진 수주 인콰이어리 지속 증가 추세, 8/10 수주 이후에도 추가 증가 중 
  인콰이어리가 모두 수주로 이어지진 않지만 증설 결정에는 충분한 수요라는 판단 
3) 발전용 엔진 인콰이어리 믹스 신규 인콰이어리 기준 육상용과 FDC 향 6:4~5:5 비율

  양사 합산 증설 규모 4.5GW 추정,  
  경쟁사와의 라인업은 동사와 유사, 대출력 엔진 9MW, 20MW 급 위주 
5) 공장별 혼용 생산 가능 여부 기존 울산 공장 테스트 베드는 3-5MW 급 선박용에 최적화로 선박용 생산에 집중 계획 
  영암 공장 및 신규 공장은 모든 종류 엔진 생산 가능 
6) 바르질라 AM 사업과의 수익성 비교 바르질라와 유사하게 LTSA(장기유지보수계약) 구조로 진행될 가능성 높음 
  수익성 역시 바르질라와 유사한 수준 기대

covering all of Australia – fell 1.1% in

and 1.4% in July. Annual growth
stalled flat with prices nationally now
5.2% below their peak in March.

with Melbourne recording a slightly

pronounced declines with prices now
down 7%yr and 6.2%yr respectively.

of sales, in rolling 3mth terms, down

forecasts are based are reasonable, the forecasts may be affected by incorrect assumptions or by known or unknown risks and uncertainties. The results ultimately achieved may differ substantially

2
WESTPAC ECONOMICS
BULLETIN
The Cotality home value index, covering all of Australia,
fell 1.1% in September with downward revisions to the
previous two months again shaving a further 0.4% off prices.
The correction has now seen prices decline 5.2% from their

March peak. Annual growth is flat.
The price correction remains broad, Cotality reporting 97% of
capital city suburbs experienced falls over the three months
to September, Darwin the only city to record gains. Sydney
and Melbourne continue to see more pronounced declines,
albeit moderating a touch, particularly in Melbourne.
Against this, declines in Brisbane, Adelaide and Perth have
accelerated over the last few months. Across segments, unit
prices and lower price tiers continue to track slightly milder

falls than houses and top tiers.
Seasonal variations accentuated price weakness through winter
but are now neutral. The peak-to-trough price decline nationally
is slightly milder at 4.6% in seasonally adjusted terms. Unless
specified, figures in titles, tables and the commentary below are
in non-seasonally adjusted terms, consistent with the ‘headline’
figures reported by Cotality. However, figures shown in charts
are in seasonally adjusted terms.

Preliminary estimates of turnover show rolling 3mth volumes
down 3.9%qtr in September and 17.4%yr with previous
estimates revised 1-2ppts lower. The monthly pattern
suggests small gains in July and August have given way to
another sizeable fall in September.
Looking at the price performance across different jurisdictions:
• Sydney prices dropped 1.4% fall in September, monthly
declines averaging 1.6% over the previous four months,
tracking a 17% annualised pace of decline since May. All

segments and tiers recorded falls in Q3 but with top and
middle tier houses notably weaker.
• Melbourne prices fell 0.7%mth, a slower pace than other
capitals. This likely reflects the weaker starting point coming
into the correction, prices already back to the levels seen in
mid-2021. As elsewhere, 'top tier' houses prices are leading
the decline. 'Low tier' unit prices mostly held their ground in
Q3, prices in this sub-segment already plumbing 10yr lows.

• Brisbane was the weakest capital city price-wise, recording
a 1.5% drop in September with previous monthly declines
also revised down heavily (August from 1% to 1.6% and
July from 1.2% to 1.7%). The combined effect has taken
the rolling 3mth change to –4.7%qtr from –2.7%qtr last
month and –0.6%qtr in July. At 5.9%yr, annual growth is
still positive but losing altitude quickly, having run at 18%yr
as recently as May. Sellers have been slow to respond to

the sudden stop with total listings up over 50% vs a year

• Adelaide dwelling prices fell 1.3%mth, the August decline

• The smaller capitals were again more mixed, price growth
still positive in Darwin (0.4%mth, 11.9%yr), relatively
resilient in Hobart (–0.5%mth, 7%yr) but weaker in
Canberra (–1.1%mth, –1.6%yr).
• Regional markets showed a 0.9% decline in Qld but slightly
milder declines in NSW (–0.7%) and Victoria (0.3%). Annual
prices growth is in the 3.4-6.3%yr range.

Our recently released Housing Pulse report (here) provides
a deeper diver into the situation in Australia's housing
markets. While the absence of selling pressure, still tight
supply-demand balance and sentiment mix suggests the
correction was still relatively 'orderly' through the September
quarter, the latest monthly data shows momentum remains
unambiguously negative.
That said, there are clearly more headwinds to come. The
RBA latest interest rate rise and what now looks to be a

more likely prospect of a follow-up increase at its meeting in
early November will impact in coming months. Next week's
consumer sentiment update will show how the change, and
the latest round of weak updates, is shaping housing-related
sentiment. Either way, momentum looks set to remain weak
into year-end and, barring a significantly improved inflation
and interest rate outlook, early 2027.

## Document 181: Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-16)

Source PDF: all report pdf/Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August (2026-10-16).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-10-16
Pages: 13
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#trade #australia
Australia’s Trade in Goods Account, August
By Ryan Wells
Economist, Westpac
13:19 October 01 2026 Share
Another surge in data centre-related equipment imports in August, alongside continued
volatility in gold trade. Trade balance: $0.5bn surplus. Export values: 3.7%mth. Import
values: 5.8%mth.

### Page 2

Australia’s goods trade surplus narrowed to $0.5bn in August from $1.4bn in July (revised down from
$1.9bn previously). This result was significantly lower than expected – the market consensus was for a
broadly stable reading, while Westpac had a slight widening pencilled in. Over the past six months or
so, the trade balance has been oscillating between small deficits and surpluses, largely coinciding with
significant month-to-month volatility in the trade mix. The chief culprit behind this volatility has been

trade in non-monetary gold, among other components.
Before discussing the detail, it is once again worth noting the results for gold up front. In August, non-
monetary gold exports jumped 20%, while imports surged 91%. Excluding gold, the trade figures have
been much more stable, closer to a deficit of $2-3bn per month over the past three months, down from
larger deficits around the start of the year.
Export earnings rose 3.7% in August, bouncing back from a 3.6% decline in July. The trend for exports

so far this year looks a bit more positive, but again, this is tricky to read given the volatility present in
the data. Excluding gold, export earnings were up 1.6% in August. This was largely supported by a
4.7% lift in coal and a 2.8% increase in LNG, with further detail provided by the ABS suggests that an
increase in both volumes and prices contributed. However, iron ore (–1.5%) and rural goods (–1.0%)
weighed on earnings in the month.

### Page 3

The import bill meanwhile increase by 5.8% in August. Once again, a surge in automatic data
processing (ADP) equipment imports – which capture the investment upswing related to data centres –
were in the spotlight, surging 79% in the month up to $4.3bn. There were also an increase in other
capital goods imports centred on telecommunications equipment (+12%) and civil aircraft (+30%) –
large in their own right but dwarfed by the surge in ADP imports in the month.

Our latest estimates have the total pipeline for data centres at around $175bn over the coming decade,
with much of that being front-loaded. It is natural to expect month-to-month volatility in ADP equipment
imports as these large and lumpy data centre projects are worked through, but it is worth highlighting
that August’s jump closely resembles the surge from March, when imports also surged to around
$4.3bn. Given the scale of the increase in August, Q3 is shaping up to be another quarter where data

centre investment will play a significant role in driving growth – while it is true that the data centre build-
out is import-intensive, our modelling suggests that once spending enters Australia, the local economic
impact is likely to be around half of the total investment.

### Page 4

Elsewhere across the import basket, while gold imports also surged, it was effectively offset by a
decline in consumer imports of non-industrial transport equipment (i.e. cars), down 21.2% in August
following the surge from May to July. While still tentative at this stage, it suggests that the momentum in
EV purchases, which played a significant role in the consumer spending story in Q2, may be starting to
wane. Additionally, a further pull-back in fuel imports (–5.0%) in August has extended the downward

trend in recent months, following the initial surge in oil prices in March and April after the war in the
Middle East began. However, with the conflict re-escalating recently and margins on refined fuel
products widening, we could see this trend reverse over the coming months.

## Document 199: CGコードが問う「取締役会の実効性」―「形式」から「実質」へ ～資本配賦・事業ポートフォリオ改革を巡る監督機能～

Source PDF: all report pdf/CGコードが問う「取締役会の実効性」―「形式」から「実質」へ ～資本配賦・事業ポートフォリオ改革を巡る監督機能～.pdf
Report source: DLRI
Date: 2026-09-29
Pages: 6
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

My Opinion

2026 年 9 月 29 日

1/6 
CG コードが問う「取締役会の実効性」―「形式」から「実質」へ 
～資本配賦・事業ポートフォリオ改革を巡る監督機能 ～ 
シニア・フェロー（非常勤）  水口 啓子 
（℡：050-5469-1527）

（要旨） 
○ 2026 年7 月に公表・施行された改訂コーポレートガバナンス・コード（CG コード）（以下、
コード）は、ガバナンス改革の実質化を重視し、形式的な対応にとどまらない、本質的なガ
バナンスの構築・運営を促している。プライム市場・スタンダード市場の上場会社について
は、コードの対象となる各原則について、実施するか、実施しない場合にはその理由を説明
するコンプライ・オア・エクスプレインが求められており、改訂コードを反映したコーポレ
ートガバナンス報告書の提出期限は2027 年 7 月末日となっている。 
○ コードでは、単なる制度や体制の記載にとどまらず、それらが実効性のある取締役会運営や
企業価値向上にどのようにつながっているのか、自社の経営環境や課題に即して説明するこ
とが求められる。コードは、単にコンプライとするだけでなく、具体的な取組みの内容や、
原則を実施していると評価する合理的な理由を説明することも、建設的な対話に資するとし
ている。 
○ コードは、取締役会の役割・責務として、成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む経
営資源の配分について、具体的な取組みを説明し、その配分が経営戦略に照らして適切かを
継続的に検証することを重視している。その実践に当たっては、どのリスクを取って成長を
目指し、どのリスクを抑制するかを取締役会が見極めることが重要となる。具体的には、設
備投資、研究開発、人的資本、無形資産等への成長投資と経営資源の配分について検証する
ことを求めるとともに、リスク管理体制に関する考慮事項として、サイバーセキュリティ、
地政学的要因によるサプライチェーン途絶、技術等の情報流出への対応を明記している。 
○ また、 こうした取締役会の機能強化に向け、コードの解釈指針では、独立社外取締役の役割 ・
責務、質・数・独立性の確保を重視するとともに、審議を活性化する観点から、取締役会事
務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機能強化が追記された。 
○ 上場企業を取り巻く事業環境の非連続な変化に伴う新たな事業機会・リスクへの迅速な対応
の重要性が高まる中で、今回の改訂は、企業と投資家双方の取組みを通じてガバナンス改革
の実質化を促している。本稿では、コードの全体像を俯瞰した上で、取締役会の実効的な監
督が経営判断にどのように寄与し得るかを踏まえて、投資家との質の高い対話へどのように
つなげ得るかを考察する。

### Page 2

2/6 
1. はじめに 
2026 年 7 月 21 日に、コーポレートガバナンス・コード（CG コード）（以下、コード）の改訂版
が公表・施行された。2015 年の導入以降、2018 年、2021 年と改訂を重ねてきたコードは、今回の
改訂によって一つの転換点を迎えたと考えられる。改訂を経てコードは、 ガバナンス体制のあり方
や具体的な対応を細かく規定するのではなく、 各社が自らの経営課題や事業環境に即して、 原則の
趣旨・精神に沿ったガバナンスを実践することを重視する方向へ軸足を移す内容となっている。事
業環境が非連続に変化し、その変化のサイクルも短期化する中、 迅速な経営判断の必要性が高まっ
ている。コーポレートガバナンスを「形式的な体制整備」から「経営判断の質を高め、中長期的な
企業価値向上につなげる仕組み」へと捉え直すものといえる。コードの全体像と主な論点は、以下
の通りである（資料１）。

資料 1 2026 年改訂コードの構成と主な論点・着眼点 
基本原則 主なテーマ 主な論点・着眼点 
基本原則 1 
株主の権利・平等性 の
確保、株主との対話 
株主が判断に必要な情報を得て権利を行使できる環境を整える。特に、
有価証券報告書の株主総会前提出を重要な例として示すとともに、少数
株主の利益保護、政策保有株式の検証、株主との建設的な対話を重
視。 
基本原則 2 
株主以外のステークホ
ルダーとの適切な協働 
持続的な成長を支える従業員や取引先等との協働を重視。解釈指針で
は、人的資本への投資と適切な分配、公正・適正な取引を例示。多様性
の確保や企業年金のアセットオーナーとしての機能発揮も対象。 
基本原則 3 
適切な 情 報 開 示 と 透
明性の確保 
財務情報に加え、戦略・リスク・ガバナンス等の非財務情報を、ひな型的
な記述にとどめず、正確で分かりやすく有用な形で示すことを重視。開示
を株主との建設的な対話の基盤と位置付ける。 
基本原則 4 取締役会等の責務 
成長の道筋を示し、成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む経営
資源配分を説明・継続的に検証することを重視。独立社外取締役による
監督、取締役会事務局の機能強化、サイバー・地政学リスク等への対応
を通じて取締役会の実効性を高める。 
（出所）東京証券取引所「コーポレートガバナンス・コード～会社の持続的な成長と中長期的な企業価値の向上のために～」
（2026 年 7 月 21 日）を参照の上、第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

今回の改訂では、 取締役会が成長の道筋を示し、 成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む
経営資源の配分について、 具体的な取組みを説明するとともに、 その妥当性を継続的に検証するこ
とが重視されている。具体的には、①取締役会の在るべき姿について不断の検討を行うこと、②独
立社外取締役の質と実効性を高めること、③取締役会事務局（コーポレートセクレタリー等）の機
能を強化することなどが重要なテーマとなっている。なお、 今回の改訂では対話環境の整備として

### Page 3

3/6 
有価証券報告書の総会前開示なども明記されている。 
企業価値を左右する経営上の重要課題について、 取締役会がどのような問いを投げかけ、 経営陣
との議論を通じて意思決定の質の向上につなげるかが重要である。 今後は、 コーポレートガバナン
ス報告書においても、実質的なガバナンスに関する記載への注目が高まることが想定される。 
本稿では、取締役会の実効性を「取締役会における議論が、経営上の重要な意思決定の質や、企
業の持続的価値創造にどのように寄与し得るか」 という観点から捉える。2026 年に改訂されたコー
ドの全体像を踏まえた上で、取締役会における「監督」の在り方を整理し、その具体的な実践領域
として、資本配賦と事業ポートフォリオ選択のあり方を取り上げる。さらに、独立社外取締役や取
締役会事務局の役割に触れつつ、 取締役会の実効性をいかに高め、 投資家との質の高い対話へつな
げるかを考察する。

2．取締役会による経営の方向付けと実効的な監督 
コードの改訂が示唆するように、取締役会の本質的な役割は、会社の進むべき方向を示し、経営
陣がリスクと機会を多角的に捉えて適切にリスクテイクできる環境を整えるとともに、 独立した客
観的な立場から経営を監督することにある（資料2）。

資料 2 取締役会の役割・責務に関わる諸視点 
項目 主な視点・検討事項 
企業戦略の方向付け 
・経営理念に基づき、成長戦略・経営計画を策定する。 
・資本コストを意識し、収益性・資本効率の目標を明確化する 。 
・成長投資、M&A、人的資本・知的財産への投資、事業ポートフォリオの見直しな
ど、経営資源の配分を具体的に示す。 
・中期経営計画未達の原因と対応策を検討し、株主に説明する。 
適切なリスクテイクの支援 
・経営陣の健全な起業家精神に基づく 提案を歓迎しつつ、客観的・多角的に検討
する。 
・経営資源が成長戦略に沿って有効活用されているかを継続的に検証する。 
・中長期的な企業価値向上につながる報酬制度・インセンティブを設計する。 
経営陣への実効的な監督 
・経営陣の業績を客観的に評価し、必要に応じて選解任に反映する。 
・特に CEO の後継者計画（サクセッションプラン）を取締役会が主体的に監督す
る。 
・適時・正確な情報開示を監督する。 
・支配株主や経営陣との利益相反・関連当事者取引を適切に管理する。 
内部統制・リスク管理 
・グループ全体の内部統制・リスク管理体制を整備し、運用状況を監督する。 
・サイバーセキュリティ、地政学的なサプライチェーン途絶、技術 等の情報流出な
どのリスクにも対応する。

### Page 4

4/6 
サステナビリティを巡る取組
み 
・中長期的な企業価値の向上の観点から、サステナビリティを巡る取組みについ
て基本的な方針を策定・適切な対応を行う。 
（出所）東京証券取引所「コーポレートガバナンス・コード～会社の持続的な成長と中長期的な企業価値の向上のために～」
（2026 年 7 月 21 日）を参照の上、第一ライフ資産運用経済研究所作成

実効的な取締役会を考察するに際しては、重要な戦略課題に議論を集中させ、経営陣に「有用な
問い」 を投げかけることが大いに期待される。 「何を取締役会が自ら判断し、 何を経営陣に委ねるの
か」という委任の境界を明確にし、経営陣の意思決定の前提、リスク選好、リスク抑制方針・施策
を多角的に検証することで、意思決定の質を高めることが期待される。 
中期経営計画の進捗、 主要な前提条件の変化などを継続的にモニタリングし、 計画が未達となっ
た場合には、 その要因を独立的な視点から分析して、 次の戦略や資本配賦に反映させることが肝要
である。持続的な企業価値創造に向けて、取締役会がリスクテイクを支援・監督する際も、ストレ
スシナリオを含め、 成長の源泉や市場規模の見立て、 競争優位性の持続可能性を踏まえた検討が必
要となる。加えて、上場会社のグローバル化がさらに進展する中、グループ全体のリスク管理・内
部統制の重要性は高まっている。 法的リスクや地政学リスク、 グローバル展開に伴う内部統制の実
効性に至るまで、多角的な問いを通じて、意思決定における前提、リスクなどを検証する姿勢が重
要となる。

3．資本配賦と事業ポートフォリオ改革の合理性の検証 
取締役会の監督機能が試される代表的な領域が、 資本配賦と事業ポートフォリオ改革であり、 投
資家にとっても大きな関心事項である。コードは、資本コストを意識し、収益性・資本効率の目標
を明確化することに加え、 成長投資、M&A、 人的資本・知的財産への投資、 事業ポートフォリオの見
直しなどについて言及している。取締役会は、どの時間軸で価値を生み出すのか、どの事業にどの
程度の経営資源を配分し、 リスク調整後にどの程度のリターンを見込むのかを、 全社的かつ多角的
な視点から判断することが求められる。 
事業ポートフォリオを検証する際には、ROIC（投下資本利益率）と WACC（加重平均資本コスト）
の比較が有用な判断材料となる。ただし、これらは取締役会が活用し得る指標の一例であり、その
比較のみで事業の継続・撤退を決めることは適切ではない。投資期間やリスク、将来の選択肢とし
ての価値に加え、将来の成長率、追加投資に対する限界リターン、構造改革の余地、事業間のシナ
ジー、撤退コストなども踏まえて、企業価値の向上に向けて評価することが重要である。様々な参
考指標が想定される中で、投下資本の規模を考慮した経済的利益（Economic Profit 、以下「EP」）
は、ROIC だけでは捉えにくい価値創造の絶対額を見る補助的な参考指標となり得る。

### Page 5

5/6 
資料 3 価値創造（EP：Economic Profit）の考え方

（出所）経済産業省「成長投資ガイダンス ―価値創造の拡大を通じた企業の持続的成長に向けて―」（2026 年）

EP において注目されるのは、ROIC を高めることにとどまらず、資本コストを超えて生み出され
る「価値の絶対額」に着目する点にある。ROIC が高い事業であっても、投下資本が小さければ、 当
該事業が生み出す EP の絶対額は小さくなり得る。また、成長余地のある事業において、追加投資
によってROIC がWACC を上回る状態を維持・拡大できる見込みがある場合には、 投下資本の拡大に
伴って EP の絶対額を拡大できる可能性がある。 一方で、 足元でROIC が高い事業だけに資本を集中
させることが、 必ずしも企業価値の極大化につながるとは限らない。 将来の成長余地や追加投資に
よる限界リターンの動向などを総合的に見極めることも肝要であろう。加えて、現時点で ROIC が
WACC を下回る事業についても、 直ちに撤退を決めるのではなく、 構造改革によって収益性を改善で
きる可能性を検討する余地がある。その際には、追加投資の規模や改革に要する期間・費用、他の
事業に経営資源を振り向けた場合の機会費用も踏まえて判断する必要がある。財務的な視点や定量
的なデータも踏まえ、事業戦略の妥当性を多角的に検証する取締役会での議論に期待したい。

4．独立社外取締役と取締役会事務局――実効性を支える基盤 
コードは、取締役会は社外取締役による問題提起を含め自由闊達で建設的な議論・意見交換を尊
ぶ気風の醸成に努めるべき旨を指摘している。取締役会が実効的な議論を展開するためには、 それ
を支える体制と環境の整備が不可欠である。独立社外取締役は、経営陣との間で緊張感のある信頼
関係を構築した上で、 経営陣に執行を委ねつつ、 監督や助言を通して取締役会における大局的な議
論や意思決定を行い、経営陣の説明が是認できない場合には、 反対意見を述べることも役割上必要
となる。監督と執行の間の情報の非対称性、監督と執行の境目の曖昧さ、さらには経営スピードと
監督の深さとのトレードオフなど、構造的な課題もある中で、独立社外取締役は、執行側から独立
した立場を保ちつつ、 業務執行の過度な細部に介入することなく、 適切な距離感を保って監督を行
うことが期待される。 
こうした課題に対して、 コードが言及する 「取締役会事務局 （コーポレートセクレタリー等） 」 の

### Page 6

本資料は情報提供を目的として作成されたものであり、投資勧誘を目的としたものではありません。作成時点で、第一ライフ資産運用
経済研究所が信ずるに足ると判断した情報に基づき作成していますが、その正確性、完全性に対する責任は負いません。見通しは予告
なく変更されることがあります。また、記載された内容は、第一生命保険ないしはその関連会社の投資方針と常に整合的であるとは限
りません。 
6/6 
機能強化が果たし得る役割は大きい。 強化された取締役会事務局による年間アジェンダの設計、 議
題の重要性に応じた審議時間の配分、取締役への情報提供、事前説明における論点整理、取締役会
での議論結果のフォローアップまでを一連のプロセスとして捉えることで、 取締役会の時間を企業
価値に直結する議論へ振り向ける余地が生まれる。 取締役会が個別案件の単なる承認作業から解放
され、企業戦略の方向付け、適切なリスクテイクの支援、内部統制・リスク管理などの重要テーマ
を踏まえた経営陣への実効的な監督に時間 を集中させることが可能となっているかが重要な論点
となる。 
例えば、独立社外取締役は、 「投資判断の前提となる市場成長率や競争優位性、期待ROIC は妥当
か」 「現在保有する事業を持ち続ける合理性はあるか」 「投資判断の前提が崩れた場合、いつ経営資
源の配分を見直すか」 といった問いを投げかけることができる。 こうした議論が経営資源の配分や
事業ポートフォリオの判断にどう影響したかは、 取締役会の実効性を考える上で重要な視点となる。
投資家と経営陣や社外取締役との対話においても、 「取締役会で何が議論され、どのように経営判
断に影響したか」という観点からの対話が視野に入る。

5．小括 
2026 年改訂 CG コードは、成長投資や事業ポートフォリオの見直しを含む経営資源の配分につい
て、 取締役会が戦略との整合性を継続的に検証することを重視している。 こうした役割を実効的に
果たすには、 独立社外取締役を含む取締役会の構成員が、 経営陣とは異なる視点から問いを投げか
け、議論を深めることが重要である。その結果、取締役会の意思決定プロセスそのものが重要とな
り、経営資源の配賦の規律について説明を求められる場面も増えていくことが想定される。 コード
の改訂を受けたコーポレートガバナンス報告書においても、監督と執行それぞれが果たす役割を踏
まえながら、どのように実効性のある取締役会運営がなされ得るのかについて、 説明の質が問われ
ることも考えられる。 
本コードと「スチュワードシップ・コード」は、車の両輪であり、両者が適切に相まって実効的
なコーポレートガバナンスが実現されることが期待される。コーポレートガバナンスの改善を強く
意識している長期保有の投資家は、コードの改訂を受けた開示を切り口に、取締役会が企業固有の
経営課題について問いを設定し、 その問いが持続的な企業価値向上にどう結び付いているかを確認
する機会を得ることも想定される。 
コード改訂を受けたコーポレートガバナンス報告書では、 成長戦略に沿って経営資源をどのよう
に配分し、 その妥当性を取締役会がいかに検証しているかについて、 各社の状況に即した説明が期
待される。原則を実施する場合にも、実施しない場合にも、その判断と具体的な取組みを丁寧に示
すことが、投資家との建設的な対話につながる。取締役会による監督の実効性が高まり、企業の成
長の道筋と資源配分の合理性を巡る対話が深まることを期待したい。

以上

## Document 235: First Impressions_ NZ retail card spending, September 2026 (2026-09-01)

Source PDF: all report pdf/First Impressions_ NZ retail card spending, September 2026 (2026-09-01).pdf
Report source: Westpac IQ
Date: 2026-09-01
Pages: 11
Extraction status: ok

### Page 1

#retail #newzealand
First Impressions: NZ retail card spending, September 2026
By Darren Gibbs
Senior Economist NZ
09:31 October 16 2026 Share
Retail spending disappointed in September as higher fuel prices crimped spending on nice
to haves.
Retail spending
Monthly change: -0.4% (prev: -0.9%, Westpac forecast: +0.8%)
Annual change: 0.9% (prev: 0.2%)

### Page 2

Core retail spending (excluding fuel/autos)
Monthly change: -0.7% (prev: -1.1%)
Annual change: 0.1% (prev: Flat)

Spending declines unexpectedly in September
Retail spending, as measured by electronic card payments, decreased 0.4%m/m in September
following an unrevised 0.9% decline in August. This a weaker result than we had expected based on
the recent sawtooth pattern and our own internal data. Unsurprisingly, with petrol prices rebounding
late in the month, spending on fuel increased 2.9%mth. However, core spending, which excludes
spending on fuel and vehicles, fell 0.7%m/m.
While spending on consumables (groceries) increased modestly in September, this was at the expense
of spending on nice to haves. Spending on hospitality suffered a further 2.3%mth fall following a
1.9%mth decline in August, and spending on apparel replicated the 2.0%mth decline reported in
August. Spending on durable goods also fell slightly in September (-0.4%mth).
Despite the weak outcome for the August and September months, retail spending over the September
quarter still eked out a minor 0.1% gain, while core spending increased 0.4%. Given higher prices, at
face value this points to a weak quarter for volumes (next week’s CPI report will cast more light on
pricing behaviour during the quarter). It is worth noting that total retail spending, as will be depicted in
the official quarterly retail sales survey released next month, could point to somewhat stronger growth.
Purchases of big-ticket items that often are bought on credit – such as cars, furniture and appliances –
are underrepresented in the electronic card spending survey.
Looking ahead, at least in the near term, the recent sharp rebound in fuel prices has again siphoned
money out of households’ pockets at a time when growth in wages (and labour incomes more
generally) remains weak. Therefore, we anticipate that growth in consumer spending volumes will
remain subdued – with regional variations dependent on exposure to the well-performing export sector
– even as the value of spending rises due to higher prices. What happens beyond this year will depend
on how the Middle East conflict evolves and how the RBNZ responds to the near-term spike in inflation.

Darren Gibbs, Senior Economist

M: +64 21 794 292
